This is [D-088](s13-decision-register.md#d-088)'s rule — *this project does not ship an unmeasured performance claim* — failing at its second edge: the claim **was** measured, three times, and the measurement was not propagated to every place that repeated it. **A number duplicated into four files is a number that will disagree with itself**, and the disagreement surfaces as a phantom regression at the worst moment, when someone is deciding whether to tag.

So the rate is now recorded in `.cargo/config.toml` **only**. The other three carry a pointer and no figure, and the config file says in as many words that it is the only place, and why.

**`main` was never red; one machine was.** CI passed on all five commits of the 0.8.0 line — `30762261075`, `30764173389`, `30767607046`, `30768128858`, `30770381302` — on windows-latest, macos-latest and ubuntu-latest, the last of those being B7 itself. *The gate is red on committed code* and *the gate is red on my machine* are different claims about different systems, and only the second was ever in evidence. The developer machine had been building wheels all session, which is the same confound `.cargo/config.toml` had already flagged against the 0.7.0 figure ("the variable is load, not code") — flagged, and then not applied to the next measurement taken on the same machine.

**The measurement could not have supported the conclusion at any rate.** Re-run with the two builds *interleaved* round-robin rather than in blocks, binaries invoked directly with `RUST_TEST_THREADS` set by hand (`[env]` does not apply outside cargo, and missing that measures the parallel rate instead), n = 20 per arm:

| arm | crashes |
|---|---|
| doctrine built at `v0.7.0` | 9/20 (45%) |
| doctrine built at `55b7844`, experiment 1 | 15/20 (75%) |
| doctrine built at `55b7844`, experiment 2 — **same executable** | 9/20 (45%) |
| doctrine at 0.8.0 with `v0.7.0`'s exact property set | 15/20 (75%) |
| `integrity_property_tests` (control) | 4/20, 2/20 |

**Read row three before row two.** One executable produced 75% and then 45% on consecutive experiments, and the arm with a property *removed* scored **above** the arm containing it — which cannot be a real effect and is therefore a direct reading of the noise. The band is ~30 points at n = 20. Every arm comparison in [R15](s11-s12-milestones-and-risks.md#r15)'s history at n ≤ 20 is uninformative about arms, including several taken as findings at the time. And the substantive answer falls out of row one: **a binary built before any 0.8.0 change faults at the same rate as HEAD's**, so there is nothing for [D-119](s13-decision-register.md#d-119)'s extra trigger, or [D-121](s13-decision-register.md#d-121)'s added property, to have caused.

**Two facts were established, and they are the return on the exercise.** The fault is **effectively Windows-only in practice**: in run `30770381302` the quarantined step took 46 s on macOS and 58 s on Ubuntu — single attempts — against 4 m 30 s on Windows. Seven prior measurements in that row describe a Windows access violation without once stating that the other two platforms pay nothing for it, which changes what the quarantine costs. And one **untested candidate** is now written down rather than left to be re-derived: the harness drives every case through `Builder::new_multi_thread()`, so the three connections of a `Database::open` can overlap on different workers even with libtest serialised — which is precisely the concurrency `.cargo/config.toml` says it has never been able to locate. A current-thread build scored 10/20 against 15/20. **That is inside the noise band and settles nothing**, and it is recorded as a candidate with its measurement attached, per [D-063](s13-decision-register.md#d-063)'s objection to leaving a question unmeasured *and* unstated. Resolving it needs n in the hundreds.

**The retry budget was not raised, and was never under strain.** [D-110](s13-decision-register.md#d-110) exists to stop exactly the move this episode invited — the local gate would not go green, and six attempts were already budgeted. It stays at six because CI clears it, not because the pressure was resisted.

**Rejected.** *Bisecting the 0.8.0 commits* — the plan of record, and it presumes the regression it was meant to locate; the `v0.7.0` arm answers the question in one measurement instead of seven. *Raising `--attempts` past six* — [D-110](s13-decision-register.md#d-110), and there is no failure to absorb. *Reducing the case counts to buy a lower rate* — a real loss of coverage bought against a phantom. *Switching the harness to a current-thread runtime on the 10/20 figure* — that is the ΔQ mistake of [D-122](s13-decision-register.md#d-122) in a new costume: a number that points the right way is not a result when the instrument's noise is larger than the number. *Leaving the stale `~3/25` in place and correcting only the conclusion* — the stale figure is the mechanism, not a detail of the story; a retraction that leaves it would let the same misreading recur.

<a id="d-125"></a>D-125 — the v7 → v8 rung is measured rather than estimated: ~10–13 µs per concept, **1.09×** peak disk settling to 1.00×, and `foreign_key_check` at 13–17% of the rung (0.8.0). [D-032](s13-decision-register.md#d-032), [D-036](s13-decision-register.md#d-036), [D-063](s13-decision-register.md#d-063), [D-088](s13-decision-register.md#d-088), [D-117](s13-decision-register.md#d-117), [D-118](s13-decision-register.md#d-118), [D-119](s13-decision-register.md#d-119), [D-124](s13-decision-register.md#d-124), [§4.1](s4-schema.md#41-concepts-and-per-model-embeddings), [§8](s6-s10-flows-to-dependencies.md#8-testing-strategy).

`migration_tests.rs` proves the v8 rung is **correct** — rowids carry across by value, the foreign-key suspension is necessary, a pre-existing orphan is refused — on fixtures of **four concepts**. At four rows nothing costs anything, so the rung shipped with no answer to the only two questions an operator actually has: *how long is my database unavailable*, and *how much free disk do I need before I start*. [D-088](s13-decision-register.md#d-088) refuses an unmeasured performance claim on a query path; a rung that rewrites a ledger table under the write lock is a stronger case, not a weaker one.

`examples/v8_migration_scale_probe.rs` measures it. Four scales, links at 3× concepts and a transaction_log row per write, FTS populated through the v7 triggers because the rung rebuilds it and an empty index would have produced a reassuring number about a database nobody has:

| concepts | links | log rows | before | peak | settled | migrate | fk_check | µs/concept |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1,000 | 3,000 | 4,000 | 3.6 MiB | 3.9 MiB | 3.6 MiB | 0.01 s | 0.001 s | 9.8 |
| 10,000 | 30,000 | 40,000 | 35.7 MiB | 38.9 MiB | 35.7 MiB | 0.07 s | 0.012 s | 7.4 |
| 50,000 | 150,000 | 200,000 | 181.4 MiB | 197.9 MiB | 181.5 MiB | 0.58 s | 0.076 s | 11.6 |
| 200,000 | 600,000 | 800,000 | 733.5 MiB | 797.3 MiB | 733.6 MiB | 2.65 s | 0.461 s | 13.3 |

**The outage is short and linear.** 2.7 s on a 733 MiB database, scaling with `concepts` rather than with the file. Nothing here needs a maintenance window.

**Peak disk is 1.09×, not 2×, and it settles back to 1.00×.** This is the result worth writing down, because the estimate everyone reaches for is wrong by an order of magnitude in the operator's favour. A copy-and-swap does need ~2× *of the table*, and the `links` rung's rustdoc says so — but `concepts` is a small share of a database whose bulk is `links` and `transaction_log`, so twice a small thing is a 9% bump in the file. The 1.09× is flat across four scales, which is what makes it a rule rather than a data point. And it is **transient**: after a `wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` the file is 1.00× its original size, so the rung keeps none of the space it borrows. Peak was **sampled** on a second thread at 10 ms rather than computed, because the obvious estimate assumes the copy is the peak and both the FTS rebuild and the WAL are capable of beating it.

**`foreign_key_check` costs 13–17% of the rung, and it is the part that does not scale with `concepts`.** [D-117](s13-decision-register.md#d-117) put it inside the transaction, correctly — a suspension that is not verified before the version stamp is not a suspension, it is a hole. What was not stated is that it is a **whole-database** scan: it grows with `links` and the log, so on a database with a large ledger relative to its concept count it will dominate a rung that is otherwise linear in a table it does not read. That is the one shape where this measurement's headline number would mislead, so it is named.

**What is *not* claimed.** The `links` rung (v6 → v7) still carries an estimated `roughly 2× links in peak disk` and **that estimate is still unmeasured**. It is now flagged as such in its own rustdoc rather than sitting beside a measured neighbour and borrowing its authority. Reading across would be precisely backwards: this rung is cheap *because* it rebuilds the small table, and that one rebuilds the large one.

**The fixture moved rather than being copied, and that is [D-124](s13-decision-register.md#d-124) applied the same day it was written.** The pinned v7 schema lived in `migration_tests.rs`. A probe needs the same pin, and a second copy of a pinned schema is a copy that will drift — which is the exact failure D-124 had just finished documenting, one file type over. It moved to `tests/common/v7_schema.rs`, included by `#[path]` from both. One pin, two readers: the file that checks the rung is correct and the file that measures what it costs can no longer disagree about what v7 was.

**A defect in the probe's own fixture, found and fixed before the numbers were believed.** The first run reported 3× links and had built 1×. Link tuples were keyed `(src, tgt, KNOWS_{i % 4})` with `src = i % n`, `tgt = (i * 7 + 13) % n`, so `i` and `i + n` collided on all three — same src, same tgt because the stride is a multiple of the modulus, same type because every scale is a multiple of 4 — and an `INSERT OR IGNORE` swallowed two rows in three in silence. The type now carries the band `i / n`, and the `OR IGNORE` is gone so a collision panics. **The measurement that was almost published was of a database with a third of the edges it claimed**, and the thing that hid it was a clause added for convenience. A fixture that cannot fail loudly is a fixture whose size is a guess.

**Rejected.** *Shipping the finding as a test rather than a probe* — [D-063](s13-decision-register.md#d-063) prefers that, and it is the wrong call here: the 200k arm takes minutes to seed and the assertion worth making ("peak stays near 1.09×") is a performance claim that would go flaky in CI on shared runners. The probe follows `tgt_index_cost_probe` and `vacuum_rowid_probe` instead, and the numbers live in the rung's rustdoc where the next person to touch it will read them. *Estimating peak from table sizes instead of sampling* — the estimate is the thing being checked. *Reading the concepts measurement across to the `links` rung* — inverted, as above. *Leaving `INSERT OR IGNORE` in and asserting the count* — the assert alone would have caught it, but the clause has no business being there: nothing about this fixture wants a collision tolerated.

<a id="d-126"></a>D-126 — 0.9.0 **does** need a schema rung: `trg_concepts_guard_delete` is still unconditional in v8, a baseline re-issue cannot change it, and `verify()` would not notice (0.8.0, pre-tag re-check). [D-022](s13-decision-register.md#d-022), [D-029](s13-decision-register.md#d-029), [D-032](s13-decision-register.md#d-032), [D-036](s13-decision-register.md#d-036), [D-117](s13-decision-register.md#d-117), [D-119](s13-decision-register.md#d-119), [D-125](s13-decision-register.md#d-125), [§4.1](s4-schema.md#41-concepts-and-per-model-embeddings), [§4.6](s4-schema.md#46-the-concept-text-index--the-third-derivative-table-055-d-051), [R15](s11-s12-milestones-and-risks.md#r15).

The 0.8.0 plan carried a risk row reading *"0.9.0 needs a rung after all — Low / **High**, forfeits the shared rebuild"*, mitigated by *"B4 installed `rowid_pk` **and** the inert delete trigger, which is the full set C2 needs… Still re-check before tagging 0.8.0."* **The re-check was performed, and the row's premise is false.** It is recorded here rather than in the row alone because the reason it was false is a property of SQLite that will catch the next person too.

**What B4 actually installed was two of C2's three schema prerequisites.** Step 1 (`cold.concepts`) is C2's own work and needs nothing from v8. Step 3 (`trg_concepts_fts_delete`, installed inert) shipped in [D-119](s13-decision-register.md#d-119) and is pinned by `the_fts_delete_trigger_is_installed_and_inert`. **Step 2 did not.** `trg_concepts_guard_delete` is still unconditional — `BEFORE DELETE ON concepts BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, …) END`, no `WHEN` clause — while its two siblings `trg_links_guard_delete` and `trg_txlog_guard_delete` have been marker-gated since archival shipped. C2 requires it to join them.

**Why that is a rung and not a baseline re-issue, measured rather than assumed.** [D-032](s13-decision-register.md#d-032) makes pre-1.0 schema changes cheap by re-issuing the baseline, and the instinct is that a changed trigger rides along. It does not, for two reasons that compound:

* **`CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS` on an existing name keeps the OLD body.** Verified directly: a trigger created with `RAISE(ABORT, 'OLD unconditional guard')`, then re-issued with a `WHEN NOT EXISTS (…)` clause and a new message, leaves `sqlite_master` still holding the original. It is not an error and it is not a warning — the statement succeeds and does nothing.
* **`verify()` compares `type, name` and never bodies.** It queries `SELECT type, name FROM sqlite_master` and checks membership against `trigger_names()` and `index_names()`. A trigger whose *definition* has drifted is present, correctly named, and therefore verified.

Together: a database created by 0.8.0 and opened by 0.9.0 code would carry the unconditional guard, pass migration verification in silence, and then **refuse concept archival at the trigger** — the one operation the release exists to add. Nothing in the ladder as it stands would say why.

**The impact is smaller than the row estimated, and the correction runs the other way.** The row rated this *High — forfeits the shared rebuild*, on the reading that the fix would have had to ride B4's `concepts` table rebuild. It does not: swapping a trigger is `DROP TRIGGER` then `CREATE TRIGGER`, no table copy, no data movement, and — unlike [D-117](s13-decision-register.md#d-117)'s rung — **no `suspends_foreign_keys`**, because no table is being rebuilt and no foreign key is transiently violated. Against [D-125](s13-decision-register.md#d-125)'s measured 2.7 s for a rung that *does* rewrite the table, this one is free. The risk was real; its cost was overestimated.

**Deliberately not folded into v8, and this is the substantive decision.** The cheap move was to ship the conditional guard in 0.8.0 and leave 0.9.0 with no rung at all. It is refused, because the gate is *the presence of `macrame_archive_session`* and that marker exists during **links** archival, which 0.8.0 already has. A conditional concepts guard in 0.8.0 would therefore leave `concepts` physically deletable for the duration of every link-archive session, in a release where nothing archives concepts and nothing wants to. That trades a real weakening of [Doctrine V](s0-s3-foundations.md#doctrine-v) — for a whole release, on the table the doctrine is least willing to lose — against saving a rung measured in milliseconds. **A guarantee is not worth a millisecond.**

**What the exit gate for that rung must do**, recorded now while the reasoning is fresh: build a genuine v8 fixture and assert the guard is *conditional* afterwards. The trap is the one `a_v7_database_climbs_to_v8_and_gains_rowid_pk` already exists to avoid — a fixture assembled from today's `ddl::` constants arrives with the change already made and would pass against a rung that does nothing. `tests/common/v7_schema.rs` ([D-125](s13-decision-register.md#d-125)) is the shape to follow, with a `v8_schema.rs` beside it.

**One general consequence, larger than this rung.** `verify()` cannot detect a drifted trigger body, so **any** future rung that changes a trigger's definition rather than its name has this same silent-success failure mode. That is worth fixing on its own terms — comparing normalised `sqlite_master.sql` against the shipped DDL text would catch it — and is not done here, because it would go red on every database whose whitespace differs from the constant and that is a change with its own design. Flagged rather than fixed, and named as the reason a `DROP` is mandatory in any such rung: **re-issuing is not updating.**

**Rejected.** *Shipping the conditional guard in v8* — weakens Doctrine V for a release, above. *Relying on the baseline re-issue* — measured false. *Leaving the row's "re-check before tagging" unresolved and tagging anyway* — the row exists because a previous release shipped with a scheduled item nobody checked ([D-113](s13-decision-register.md#d-113)); leaving this one open would be the same failure with the tripwire watching the wrong file. *Making `verify()` compare trigger bodies as part of this entry* — a real improvement and a different change, with a false-positive mode that needs designing rather than bolting on before a tag.

<a id="d-127"></a>D-127 — the §9 budget table is re-measured for 0.8.0, and the read-path gains are attributed by **two** controls rather than claimed (0.8.0, pre-tag). [D-055](s13-decision-register.md#d-055), [D-088](s13-decision-register.md#d-088), [D-090](s13-decision-register.md#d-090), [D-110](s13-decision-register.md#d-110), [D-115](s13-decision-register.md#d-115), [D-116](s13-decision-register.md#d-116), [D-118](s13-decision-register.md#d-118), [D-124](s13-decision-register.md#d-124), [§9](s6-s10-flows-to-dependencies.md#9-performance-budgets).

Three 0.8.0 items changed the read path — [D-115](s13-decision-register.md#d-115) interned `EdgeRef` against a per-`Subgraph` pool, [D-116](s13-decision-register.md#d-116) took `content` out of the default load, [D-118](s13-decision-register.md#d-118) dropped an index off `links_current` — and the published table was measured at 0.7.0. [D-088](s13-decision-register.md#d-088) forbids shipping an unmeasured performance claim; a table carried across three representation changes is one.

| Operation | Budget | 0.7.0 | 0.8.0 | |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Single assertion | ≤ 5 ms | — | 258 µs | still O(out-degree) |
| Chunk commit (edges, 90 rows) | ≤ 3 ms | 2.39 ms | 2.40 ms | **untouched control** |
| Three-hop traversal | ≤ 10 ms | 2.1 ms | **1.66 ms** | −21% |
| Vector top-10 | ≤ 20 ms | 294 µs | **246 µs** | −16% |
| Hybrid top-10 | ≤ 50 ms | 2.0 ms | **1.77 ms** | −12% |
| Full fold (reconstruct) | ≤ 100 ms | 21 ms | **16.9 ms** | −20% |
| Composition (snapshot + delta) | ≤ 100 ms | 3.4 ms | **2.18 ms** | −36% |
| `control/select_1` | — | 1.589–1.639 µs | 1.51–1.62 µs | **machine control** |

**The controls are the entry, not the percentages.** Five unrelated read paths improving by 12–36% at once is *also* precisely what a faster machine, a warmer cache or a quieter session looks like, and [D-124](s13-decision-register.md#d-124) had just finished documenting what happens when this project reads a number against a baseline it cannot vouch for. So the gains are only claimed against two independent controls:

* **The machine control.** [D-090](s13-decision-register.md#d-090) put a fixed trivial operation in every bench group for exactly this. It reads 1.51–1.62 µs today against the 1.589–1.639 µs recorded there — unchanged, so the hardware and the session are comparable.
* **The untouched-path control**, which is the stronger of the two and was not designed as one. Chunk commit at 90 edges goes **2.39 → 2.40 ms**. Nothing in 0.8.0 touched the chunked write path, and it did not move. A machine-wide speedup would have moved it too.

Machine unmoved, untouched path unmoved, read paths moved. That triangulates the gain onto the code, which no single arm does.

**The single-assertion row changed shape and the caveat is the load-bearing half.** It now carries a number (258 µs on this fixture) where it previously carried only *"not met at high out-degree"*. Both are true and the second still governs: [D-118](s13-decision-register.md#d-118)'s index drop bought −7.9% on that path and **did not change the complexity** — the single-open guard still scans the source's whole out-degree ([D-059](s13-decision-register.md#d-059)), so a high-degree hub still exceeds 5 ms. A measured figure beside an unchanged complexity claim is an invitation to read the figure as the answer, so the row states both and the README repeats the caveat rather than the number alone.

**These are still not gates**, and 0.8.0 does not change that ([D-055](s13-decision-register.md#d-055)). An absolute `≤ 5 ms` asserted on a shared CI runner is an assertion about whichever machine picked up the job — the flaky red this project refuses by name elsewhere. Regression detection remains criterion baselines, machine against itself.

**R15 reaches the benches too, and the run had to be restructured around it.** `cargo bench --bench budgets` died at `0xC0000005` inside `bulk_chunks` and took every later group with it — benches open databases, so they are exposed exactly as the test binaries are, which this project had not previously recorded. Criterion prints each measurement as it completes, so a crash loses the *remainder* of the run rather than corrupting what printed; the benches are therefore run **one group per process**, so a fault costs one group instead of all of them, retrying only on `0xC0000005` for the reason [D-110](s13-decision-register.md#d-110) gives. `chunk_budget` needed one retry.

**A harness defect caught by a repeat of the same mistake.** The first parser matched a benchmark id and its `time:` on one line; criterion prints them on two. It found nothing and reported four groups as `ok` **with no measurements** — the silent-empty-pass shape [D-110](s13-decision-register.md#d-110) exists to catch one binary over, and it very nearly produced a "re-measured" table with no measurement behind it. Noted because it is the third instance this release: a gate that cannot distinguish *nothing ran* from *everything passed* will eventually report the first as the second.

**Rejected.** *Publishing the improvements on the numbers alone* — a uniform gain is the signature of a machine change, and one arm cannot tell them apart. *Dropping the "not met at high out-degree" caveat now that the row has a number under budget* — the fixture is not the hazard, the complexity is. *Making the budgets CI gates now that they all pass comfortably* — [D-055](s13-decision-register.md#d-055); passing on this machine is not the property a gate would assert. *Re-running the whole bench binary until it survives* — it is a 20-minute run against a fault at roughly one in three, and per-group execution is both faster and more informative about which group faulted.

<a id="d-128"></a>D-128 — concept archivability is **reachability**, not a closed interval; it reads **both** clocks, and the property that checks it needed four concepts before it could fail (0.9.0, C1). [D-012](s13-decision-register.md#d-012), [D-022](s13-decision-register.md#d-022), [D-029](s13-decision-register.md#d-029), [D-030](s13-decision-register.md#d-030), [D-080](s13-decision-register.md#d-080), [D-126](s13-decision-register.md#d-126), [Doctrine V](s0-s3-foundations.md#doctrine-v), [Doctrine VII](s0-s3-foundations.md#doctrine-vii), [§5.7](s5-modules.md#57-temporalarchivers--cold-storage).

A link assertion has a closed interval, so `LINKS_ARCHIVABLE` can ask whether the interval ended. **A concept is an entity and has no closed state.** `retired = 1` says belief in it stopped; it says nothing about whether anything still points at it. So archivability is expressed as reachability:

> A concept is archivable when it is `retired`, **both** its clocks are behind the cutoff, and **no surviving row of hot `links` names it in either direction.**

The two `links` foreign keys are what make the last clause load-bearing. Archiving a concept physically removes its row, and a surviving hot link naming it leaves the key unsatisfiable. `ON DELETE CASCADE` is not the escape, because the rows it would cascade onto are ledger rows — [Doctrine V](s0-s3-foundations.md#doctrine-v) refuses absence caused by a mistake, and a cascade is exactly that. Concept archival is therefore **strictly downstream of link archival**, and inside a session the predicate is evaluated *after* the `links` delete, never before it. Asked before and after one session it legitimately gives two different answers; `archiving_links_only_enlarges_the_archivable_set` pins the direction.

**Both clocks, where the specification named one.** C1 as written asked for `retired` and `valid_to < cutoff`. `recorded_at < :cutoff` is in the predicate as well, mirroring `LINKS_ARCHIVABLE`. A concept retired with its `valid_to` behind the cutoff but *recorded* at or after it is a fact the session is not yet meant to touch, and archiving it would send the concept cold while the log entries describing it stayed hot. That is the same two-clock mismatch the `links_current` compensation carried until Wave 4.5, reached from the other side — [Doctrine II](s0-s3-foundations.md#doctrine-ii): two clocks, never mixed. No clause is needed for the open sentinel: `9999-12-31T23:59:59.999999Z` sorts above every canonical stamp ([D-029](s13-decision-register.md#d-029)), so an open concept fails `valid_to < :cutoff` for any cutoff a caller can pass.

**The other two foreign keys are not clauses, and the distinction is doctrinal rather than practical.** `concepts` also has inbound keys from `analytics_annotations` and from every registered `embeddings_*` table. Neither blocks archival, because both hold **derived** rows: [Doctrine VII](s0-s3-foundations.md#doctrine-vii) makes an embedding an artifact of a model applied to content, and an annotation is the output of an algorithm that read `concepts` in the first place. A derived row is removed and recomputed; a ledger row is neither. Blocking on a recomputable artifact would answer *"not yet"* forever for any concept that had ever been embedded. C2 is where those rows are actually disposed of, and the `analytics_annotations` DDL comment still says *"concepts are never physically deleted (D-022)"* — true today, and one of the things C2 must correct rather than inherit.

**The predicate is observable before anything can act on it.** `temporal::archivable_concepts(conn, cutoff)` returns the ids, read-only. Concept archival is the one operation in this crate a caller cannot undo without a cold file to hand, so the decision it turns on is answerable on its own rather than only as a count in a report afterwards.

## The evidence was wrong three times before the predicate was ever in doubt

The property is the model-based shape [D-030](s13-decision-register.md#d-030) requires: generate histories, compute archivability independently in Rust, demand set equality in both directions. It passed on the first run. **It was then injected with five defects and caught two.**

| Injection | 2 concepts | + target-only `c2` | + unreferenced `c3` |
|---|---|---|---|
| `retired = 1` → `1 = 1` | **passed** | **passed** | caught |
| reachability checks `source_id` only | **passed** | caught | caught |
| drop `valid_to < :cutoff` | caught | caught | caught |
| drop `recorded_at < :cutoff` | **passed** | caught | caught |
| `NOT EXISTS` → `EXISTS` | caught | caught | caught |

The cause is the same each time and it is a property of the *generator*, not of the predicate. `CONCEPTS_ARCHIVABLE` is a conjunction of four clauses, and a conjunction can only be tested clause by clause if the generator can reach each one **independently**:

* With both endpoints drawn from the same two-id domain, a history of up to twelve edges names both concepts in nearly every case. `NOT EXISTS` is false for both, the archivable set is empty on both sides, and the three row-level clauses are never evaluated at all.
* Adding `c2`, which may be a link *target* but never a *source*, is the only shape that separates "appears as a source" from "appears at all". Without it those two coincide and the narrowed reachability check changes no answer.
* Adding `c3`, which no generated edge may name, makes the row-level clauses live on **every** case rather than on the ~1-in-10 where nothing happened to reference `c2`. That is what 32 cases cannot supply by luck, and it is why `retired` survived two rounds.

All five injections fail against the four-concept generator, at 32 cases and again at 512.

**The general lesson is worth more than the fix, and it is the third instance of one shape this cycle.** The predicate was correct throughout; what was wrong was the *evidence* for it. [D-124](s13-decision-register.md#d-124) was a number read against a baseline that could not support it, [D-127](s13-decision-register.md#d-127) was a bench parser that reported four groups `ok` with no measurements, and this is a property test that passed without exercising three of the four things it claimed to check. Each is a gate that could not distinguish *nothing was tested* from *everything passed*. **A green property test over a conjunction is not evidence until each clause has been shown to be able to fail on its own** — which costs one injection run per clause, and is now the standard this project holds archive predicates to.

**Rejected.** *Blocking archivability on `analytics_annotations` and `embeddings_*` for symmetry with `links`* — it conflates derived rows with ledger rows and would make any embedded concept permanently unarchivable. *`ON DELETE CASCADE` on the `links` foreign keys*, which would make the reachability clause unnecessary — it deletes ledger rows to satisfy a constraint, which is erasure arriving through the back door ([§2](../Macrame%20Implementation%20Plan%20v0.8.0-v0.9.0.md)). *Taking the specification's `valid_to`-only form* — it mixes the two clocks against the cutoff in a way `LINKS_ARCHIVABLE` deliberately does not. *Evaluating the predicate before the link delete so one session's answer is stable* — the downstream relationship is the design, and freezing the answer early would archive strictly less for no benefit. *Widening `NODES` from two to four for the whole binary rather than adding a separate constant* — the tiny domains are load-bearing for the collision-seeking properties above it ([D-030](s13-decision-register.md#d-030)), and widening them would weaken four tests to strengthen two. *Leaving the property at 2 concepts and raising `PROPTEST_CASES`* — the missing shapes are unreachable in that generator at any case count, which is the point: this was not a sampling problem.

<a id="d-129"></a>D-129 — schema v9: the concepts delete guard becomes marker-gated, and `verify` starts reading trigger **bodies** (0.9.0, C2). [D-008](s13-decision-register.md#d-008), [D-022](s13-decision-register.md#d-022), [D-032](s13-decision-register.md#d-032), [D-036](s13-decision-register.md#d-036), [D-117](s13-decision-register.md#d-117), [D-119](s13-decision-register.md#d-119), [D-126](s13-decision-register.md#d-126), [§4.6](s4-schema.md#46-the-concept-text-index--the-third-derivative-table-055-d-051).

**A corrective entry, not an architectural one.** It ships no capability of its own: it closes the hole [D-126](s13-decision-register.md#d-126) found during the 0.8.0 pre-tag re-check, so that [D-130](s13-decision-register.md#d-130)'s partition has somewhere to land. `trg_concepts_guard_delete` now carries the same `WHEN NOT EXISTS` probe of `macrame_archive_session` its two siblings have had since 0.5.3 ([D-008](s13-decision-register.md#d-008) revised). An ad-hoc `DELETE` is still refused; it is refused for the reason `links` is, rather than because concept archival is impossible.

**Two statements, and the cost is independent of database size.** A `DROP TRIGGER` and a `CREATE TRIGGER` — no table rebuild, no data movement, no `suspends_foreign_keys` ([D-117](s13-decision-register.md#d-117) is not involved, because nothing here touches a table with inbound keys). It is the cheapest rung this ladder has, and the contrast with [D-125](s13-decision-register.md#d-125)'s measured v7 → v8 rung is worth keeping: *a rung is not expensive by virtue of being a rung.* What made v7 → v8 cost 10–13 µs per concept was the table rebuild, not the version bump.

**The `IF NOT EXISTS` behaviour is now asserted against the engine rather than argued.** D-126 concluded a rung was necessary because `CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS` on an existing name keeps the **old body**. That is a claim about libSQL, not about this crate, so `re_issuing_the_baseline_guard_keeps_the_v8_body` states it as a test. If the engine ever changes its mind the test fails, and the correct response is to reconsider whether the rung is needed — not to update the test.

## The second hole, which is the half D-126 could only describe

`verify` compared `type` and `name` and never bodies. A guard with the right name and a pre-v9 body therefore passed verification in silence, and then refused archival at the trigger. Presence was never the property that mattered for these three triggers: **a guard with the right name and the wrong body is a guard that refuses a legal archive or permits an illegal delete**, and a name check cannot see the difference.

`verify` now requires every delete guard's body to contain `macrame_archive_session`. It checks for the marker name and **not** for the trigger's full text, deliberately: a whitespace-sensitive comparison that has to be hand-updated whenever a guard is reworded is a check people switch off, and it would fail on cosmetic edits while still missing a semantic one. What is asserted is the single property all three share and none may lose — *this guard is gated on the archive session*.

**A pin the rung above needed and did not have, found while writing this one.** [`add_concepts_rowid_pk`](../../src/schema/migrations.rs) rebuilds `concepts` and restores its triggers by looping over `CREATE_TRIGGERS`, which is *today's* DDL. Left alone, the v7 → v8 rung would have installed the v9 guard body, so a database the ladder reported as v8 would not have been a v8 database. `CONCEPTS_GUARD_DELETE_V8` pins it. This is unobservable in the common path, because `run` never rests at an intermediate version — a v7 file climbs 7 → 8 → 9 in one call. It is pinned anyway: **a rung is a statement about the past, and a rung that quietly writes the present into it cannot be tested against a fixture**, because the fixture and the rung would be reading the same constant. That is the trap the plan flagged for this item, discovered one rung earlier than expected.

**Three tripwires fired and all three were right.** `a_version_bump_must_bring_its_own_rung_test` by design — this rung brought four. `refusal_reason` asserted that a failure's `to` equals `SCHEMA_VERSION`, which held only while the last rung happened to be the top one; a refusal partway up the ladder correctly names its own rung's target, and the helper was over-constrained rather than the ladder wrong. And the v7 climb needed its `links` deleted before its `concepts`, which is [D-128](s13-decision-register.md#d-128)'s downstream relationship reached from the schema side rather than the predicate side.

**And one test that should have gone red did not.** `the_fts_delete_trigger_is_installed_and_inert` predicted *in its own comment* that it would fail when the guard became conditional. It did not. It only ever attempted an *ad-hoc* delete, which is still refused at v9 — so the assertion stayed true while the sentence explaining it stopped being. **A test can outlive its own reasoning without failing**, which is why the reasoning is worth re-reading whenever the thing it depends on moves; nothing mechanical will prompt it. Rewritten to assert both sides, and the second half — that `trg_concepts_fts_delete` fires inside a session — could not have been written before this rung, because there was no legal concept delete to write it with.

While rewriting it the `#[tokio::test]` attribute was dropped, and **the only signal was the suite total going 37 → 36 with nothing red.** Recorded because it is the same shape as [D-110](s13-decision-register.md#d-110), [D-124](s13-decision-register.md#d-124) and [D-127](s13-decision-register.md#d-127): a gate that cannot distinguish *nothing ran* from *everything passed*. Four instances in two releases is a pattern rather than a coincidence.

**Rejected.** *Making the guard conditional in v8 rather than taking a rung* — refused in [D-126](s13-decision-register.md#d-126) and the reason still holds: the marker exists during *links* archive sessions too, so shipping the conditional guard early would have left concepts deletable during them for a whole release. *Re-issuing the baseline instead of a rung* — measured false; the old body survives. *Comparing full trigger text in `verify`* — brittle against rewording, and a check that fails on whitespace gets disabled. *Verifying every trigger's body rather than the three guards'* — the other triggers' bodies are not version-significant in the same way, and widening the check would turn every cosmetic DDL edit into a migration failure on existing databases. *Skipping the `CONCEPTS_GUARD_DELETE_V8` pin because `run` never stops at v8* — true today and an accident of the ladder having no other consumer; the pin costs one constant and makes the rung testable.

<a id="d-130"></a>D-130 — what crosses the archive boundary: **entity data moves, derived data is disposed of**, and `content` crosses with the row (0.9.0, C2). [D-012](s13-decision-register.md#d-012), [D-022](s13-decision-register.md#d-022), [D-036](s13-decision-register.md#d-036), [D-116](s13-decision-register.md#d-116), [D-119](s13-decision-register.md#d-119), [D-128](s13-decision-register.md#d-128), [D-129](s13-decision-register.md#d-129), [Doctrine III](s0-s3-foundations.md#doctrine-iii), [Doctrine V](s0-s3-foundations.md#doctrine-v), [Doctrine VII](s0-s3-foundations.md#doctrine-vii), [§5.7](s5-modules.md#57-temporalarchivers--cold-storage).

`concepts` has four inbound foreign keys, and the archive treats them as two kinds:

| | crosses? | why |
|---|---|---|
| the `concepts` row itself | **moved, column for column** | it is the entity |
| `links` (×2) | already cold, or the concept is not archivable | [D-128](s13-decision-register.md#d-128)'s reachability clause |
| `analytics_annotations` | **deleted** | derived; recomputable by re-running the algorithm |
| `embeddings_*` | **deleted** | derived ([Doctrine VII](s0-s3-foundations.md#doctrine-vii)); also uncarryable — `F32_BLOB` and DiskANN are libSQL-specific |

**`content` crosses, and the argument is Doctrine V rather than convenience.** Archival is a move, not a rewrite, and **a move that drops a column is a rewrite**. The transaction-log payload for a concept carries its `content` ([§4.3](s4-schema.md#43-the-transaction-log)), so a cold concept whose text had been dropped would contradict `cold.transaction_log` about itself: the log says there was text, the row says there is none. Rehydration would then return a concept the ledger never recorded — precisely the absence [Doctrine V](s0-s3-foundations.md#doctrine-v) says must be explicable by the ledger and never caused by a mistake.

**The tension with [D-116](s13-decision-register.md#d-116) is apparent rather than real, and it is worth stating because it will be re-raised.** D-116 took `content` out of the default load: most readers do not want document text and paying for it by default was the wrong shape. That is a decision about the **in-memory** `NodeData` representation. This is **on-disk** storage. Disk carries the text; memory does not populate it until asked. Two independent defaults, and collapsing them would make rehydration lossy in order to save a read nobody was performing.

**The disposal is not incidental to the move — it is what makes the move legal.** `analytics_annotations` and `embeddings_*` hold foreign keys into `concepts`, and would refuse the `DELETE` outright. `ON DELETE CASCADE` is rejected here as it is everywhere in this schema, but the objection is narrower than it first looks and [§5.7](s5-modules.md#57-temporalarchivers--cold-storage)'s original wording was imprecise: the problem with a cascade is the **silence**, not the destruction. An embedding is recomputable from content, so removing it as a declared step of an archive session and recomputing it on rehydration is sound; having a foreign key quietly delete it as a side effect of something else is not. `embeddings_*` is enumerated from `sqlite_master` rather than from a list, because the set is whatever `register_model` created on *this* database.

**Nothing here is logged, and that is the decision rather than an omission.** `concepts` carries log triggers on insert and update but none on delete, and this rung does not add one. Archival mints no transaction-time facts: the concept is in the cold file, the log entries describing it are either still hot or in `cold.transaction_log`, and nothing about what was believed — or when — has changed. A log row written here would assert that something happened to the concept at archive time, which is exactly the lie [Doctrine III](s0-s3-foundations.md#doctrine-iii) exists to prevent. This is the same conclusion [§2.3](../Macrame%20Implementation%20Plan%20v0.8.0-v0.9.0.md) reaches for rehydration, arrived at from the archival side.

## C2's step 4 was a no-op, and it was tested rather than assumed

The plan asks that `reconstruct` fold cold concepts by the same last-writer-wins `seq_id` rule used for the log. **That step assumes the fold reads the `concepts` table. It does not.** `reconstruct` is driven entirely by `transaction_log`, whose `'I'` and `'U'` payloads carry the concept's fields, and archival moves no log rows for a concept — so the answer is unchanged across the boundary with no new code at all.

This is recorded rather than quietly skipped because it is a fact about the architecture that the plan assumed otherwise, and it is exactly the kind of thing someone re-derives in six months and wonders whether it is still true. `reconstruct_gives_the_same_answer_across_an_archive` was written **to find out** rather than to confirm: it compares the full `MaterializedState` on either side of a real archive, and it would fail the day the fold acquires a dependency on the hot `concepts` table. The log already pays for this; the test is what keeps that answer checkable.

**`rowid_pk` crosses, carrying a hazard C3 inherits.** It is the record of what the rowid *was*. Reinstating it is not obviously safe: `concepts.rowid_pk` is a plain `INTEGER PRIMARY KEY`, so SQLite may reuse a freed value, and archiving the highest rowids can leave a later insert holding one a cold row still claims. The two exits are named in the DDL comment so C3 meets both: **reinstate the original when it is still free, or assign a fresh one and update `concepts_fts`'s `content_rowid` mapping to match** — the index is external-content keyed on that column ([D-119](s13-decision-register.md#d-119)), and a rehydration that reassigns without re-pointing leaves the search index silently describing the wrong row.

**Five injections, and two fixtures exist only because they were not caught.** The first fixture passed an injection narrowing the reachability check to `source_id` alone, because every concept an edge named was also a source of one — so "appears as a source" and "appears at all" coincided. `target_only` is named by an edge it is only the *target* of. **This is the same hole `ARCHIVE_NODES` documents in [D-128](s13-decision-register.md#d-128), found the same way, one item later** — which suggests it is a property of how fixtures get written rather than a lapse: the natural fixture is symmetric, and asymmetry has to be deliberate. The four unarchivable concepts now each fail a *different* clause.

`recorded_at` has no fixture in that binary and cannot have one: transaction time is crate-stamped ([Doctrine II](s0-s3-foundations.md#doctrine-ii)), so every row there shares it — which also means **the cutoff must sit in the future for anything to be archivable at all**, and a test using a past cutoff would pass against a predicate with any clause deleted, the answer being the empty set either way. That clause stays covered by injection in `integrity_property_tests`, where the generator controls the clocks. A clause that can only be exercised when the caller controls the clock belongs in the property suite, and saying so is better than a seeded fixture that pretends to cover it.

**Rejected.** *Dropping `content` on the way out and recomputing from the log* — the log is archivable too, so this makes cold concept text depend on a `cold.transaction_log` that a later session may not have received; and it is a rewrite by [Doctrine V](s0-s3-foundations.md#doctrine-v). *Carrying the embeddings into `cold.concepts` for symmetry with the row* — `F32_BLOB` and DiskANN do not exist on a plain ATTACHed file, and [Doctrine VII](s0-s3-foundations.md#doctrine-vii) makes the vector recomputable, so this is cost with no property bought. *Making `analytics_annotations` block archivability instead of being deleted* — [D-128](s13-decision-register.md#d-128) rejects it for the same reason as embeddings: a concept that had ever been annotated would be permanently unarchivable. *Logging the concept delete so the archive is visible in the ledger* — it would date the concept's disappearance to archive time, which is false; the archive is a physical move and the horizon is where its visibility belongs. *Writing C2 step 4 anyway, defensively, in case the fold changes* — an unexercised fold over `cold.concepts` is untested code that looks like a guarantee.
